Krispy Kreme Is Bringing <i><em>Rudolph</em></i> To Life With A Brand-New Christmas Doughnut Collection

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Who needs a glowing red nose when you've got doughnuts this festive?

If you want to bring ho-ho-home some holiday cheer, Krispy Kreme has you covered with its brand-new Rudolph Christmas Collection. Inspired by the classic 1964 Rudolph the Red-Nosed Reindeer film, the doughnut chain is whipping up festive, sugary treats featuring some of the movie’s most beloved characters.

The newcomers are the Rudolph, Bumble, and Sam the Snowman Doughnuts. Rudolph starts with an Original Glazed doughnut dipped in icing, then gets a glittery Christmas garland sprinkle blend, vanilla-flavored frosting, and a Rudolph wreath decoration.

The Bumble Doughnut has an unglazed shell filled with raspberry filling and dipped in vanilla icing. It's finished with a vanilla frosting garland, white glitter sprinkles, and a Bumble fondant piece. Finally, the Sam the Snowman Doughnut starts with an Original Glazed dipped in vanilla icing and covered in white chocolate curls, then gets a buttercreme Christmas tree and Sam the Snowman fondant piece.

“We love giving fans new ways to enjoy the traditions they grew up with, and this collection is all about bringing people together over something fun, festive and delicious,” Alison Holder, Chief Product and Brand Officer at Krispy Kreme, said in a statement.

The new collection arrives November 28 at participating Krispy Kreme locations nationwide, just two days after the chain wraps up its Thanksgiving doughnut collection. That lineup featured four holiday-inspired flavors: Berry Cobbler, Caramel Pumpkin, Double Chocolate Crumble, and Cinnamon Apple Filled.

As for the new Rudolph treats, you can pick them up individually or grab a dozen in a special collectible-style box featuring the beloved characters.

Sylvester Stallone Wants a ‘Tulsa King’ Spinoff Starring Arnold Schwarzenegger

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The actor-producer wants his enemy-turned-friend for another Taylor Sheridan show.

Arnold Schwarzenegger stars in … Vegas King? Detroit King? Atlantic City King? Something like that?

In a new interview, Sylvester Stallone says he wants his former box-office rival-turned-friend for a Tulsa King spinoff.

“I would love in a perfect world to do an extension of Tulsa King starring [Schwarzenegger] in his own show,” Stallone told Cigar Aficionado in a cover story. “Since we’re comrades in the fantasy world, our characters meet and discuss things, like we did in The Expendables.” The actor added: “I hope he agrees.” Related Stories Movies How 'I Play Rocky' Star P.J. Byrne Became One of Hollywood's Go-To Character Actors Hollywood Flashback Yo, Academy! When 'Rocky' Was Crowned Best Picture Champ

To be clear, no such project has been announced in any stage of development, and this could easily just be Stallone floating the concept in an interview. Paramount+ and Tulsa King producer 101 Studios had no immediate comment. A Nielsen ratings hit, Tulsa King already has one spinoff, Frisco King, which stars Samuel L. Jackson.

With I Play Rocky, a biographical film about the writer-actor’s iconic breakthrough role, currently in theaters, Stallone also detailed how he fought with the original film’s director the plans for the sequel, Rocky II.

“Then I wrote the one which is where they’re married and the one that was eventually made,” Stallone said. “Well, when I wrote that, the director [John G. Avildsen] goes, ‘No, no, no—we want Rocky to go off the reservation. We want him to be drinking a lot, partying a lot.’ I go, ‘Where’s Adrian?’ He goes, ‘Oh, he got rid of her because he was going to the Playboy mansion’ … And then Rocky doesn’t fight anymore. He just becomes like a drunk and it was how a lot of fighters go off, lose it, get on drugs. I go, ‘Drugs? He doesn’t even drink for Christ’s sake.’ And he goes, ‘Well, I’m not going to do it.’ He quits.”

Stallone also cast doubt on returning as Rocky Balboa. “It would be hard…I was going to have a protégé and train him, but I think it would be more likely that they would have a prequel. That would work because it’d be so interesting. Mickey at 30 years old, Rocky at 16.”

Colts sign OT Wanya Morris off Cowboys practice squad

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Colts are adding an offensive lineman.

Wanya Morris is signing with Indianapolis’ 53-man roster off Dallas’ practice squad, ESPN’s Adam Schefter reports.

Morris, 26, was traded to the Falcons from the Chiefs in June, but was waived by Atlanta at roster cuts in August. He then joined the Cowboys in early September.

He has not appeared in a regular-season game so far in 2026.

Originally a Kansas City third-round pick, Morris has appeared in 43 career games with 16 starts.

Benjamín Naishtat’s ‘The Seven Madmen,’ With ‘I’m Still Here’ Star Selton Mello, Wraps Production

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

‘The Seven Madmen’ marks the latest from ‘Rojo’ and ‘Glaxo’ helmer Benjamín Naishtat, fast-consolidating as one of Latin America’s major directors

“The Seven Madmen,” starring Selton Mello (“I’m Still Here”) and directed by Benjamín Naishtat, is wrapping its shoot in Buenos Aires on Nov. 24, marking the latest advance on a production that weighs in as one of Latin America’s big bets for A-List festival selection in 2027.

A present day noir thriller loosely adapting Argentinean novelist Robert Arlt’s 1929 and 1931 modern classic novels “The Seven Madmen” and “The Flamethrowers,” the movie also reps the latest feature from Argentina’s Naishtat (“Rojo,” “Puan,” “Glaxo”) who is fast consolidating as one of Latin America’s major auteurs. Related Stories Netflix Orders Harlan Coben Limited Series 'The Innocent' From 'I Will Find You' Team 'KPop Demon Hunters' Golden World Tour Sets 2027 Arena Dates

Naishtat also forms part of a loose knit but burgeoning new generation of Spanish-language cineastes (think Spain’s Rodrigo Sorogoyen) that have a clear sense of film genre and combine bold visuals with a large concern for contemporary social issues.

“The Seven Madmen” also stars Martin Piroyansky (“Porno y helado”). Arlt’s novel of the same time turned on young Remo Erdosian who’s threatened with imprisonment unless he returns money he’s stolen. He seeks out Alberto, a mysterious Astrologer, who promises to help Remo if in return the young man joins his organization which, he discovers, aims to overthrow the government.

Just how much of that get into Naishtat’s film, given it is also inspired by “The Flamethrowers,” remains to be seen with its producers keeping its plot under wraps, describing it as “set in a twisted Buenos Aires” and “an intense thriller.” It is known, however, that Piroyansky plays Remo and Mello the Astrologer.

“Benjamín is a deeply inspiring filmmaker,” Mello told Variety. “It is an honor to have been invited by him to bring to life one of the most iconic characters in Argentine literature: El Astrólogo, created by Roberto Arlt and now reimagined by Benjamín in the film we are making together.”

Mello added: “I have been experiencing something new: a sense of Latin American belonging that moves me deeply while also expanding my artistic expression. This project has brought about an intimate celebration of a rare and very special moment in my journey.”

Cast is rounded up by Lorena Vega (“Envious”), Diego Cremonesi, Sofía Morandi, Andrea Frigerio, Iván González, Paula Grinszpan and Lola Berthet, with Carla Peterson (“27 Nights”) and special guest star Mercedes Morán (“The Kingdom”).

“Ever since I first read ‘The Seven Madmen,’ I have been captivated by its dark beauty, by its characters who exist beyond the reach of any law, and by its ability to capture Buenos Aires in a way perhaps no other book ever has,” said Naishtat.

He added that he had spent “more than 10 years” working on an adaptation which he knew was “impossible, which, in a way, came as a relief, since my interpretation is decidedly free.”

“At the darkest moment in Argentine cinema, devastated by a government hostile to any form of cultural expression, an eclectic group of producers from around the world, an extraordinary cast, and a world-class crew joined forces to make this project a reality.”

In an era where Latin American films of late ambition can only be made with streaming service support or multi-lateral co-production, the “eclectic group” referred to by Naishtat takes in Argentina’s Púcara Cine, founded by Federico Eibuszyc, which produced Naishtat’s “The Movement,” “Rojo” and “Puán” and Pasto, founded by Bárbara Francisco Mendivilm which teamed with Púcara to produce “Puan.”

They are joined by “Argentina 1985” producer Infinity Hill, Particular Crowd (“The Extortion”) and Brazilian production companies MyMama Entertainment (“Medusa”) and Bubbles Project (“Elephants in the Fog,”) and Ecce Films (France), Materia Cinema (Spain), and Pandora Films (Germany), in association with Bord Cadre and Sula Films.

It is no coincidence that two novels published in 1929 and 1931, an era of social fanaticism which saw the rise of fascism, are now set to receive a free present-day adaptation.

“Revisiting the unclassifiable marvel that Roberto Arlt published in 1929 is a timely endeavor in order to decipher the Argentinian and planetary present, said Naishtat.

Noted Particular Crowd President Peter Bevan: ”’The Seven Madmen’ pulsates with relentless tension, singular humor and Benjamín Naishtat’s signature lucidity.”

Superman Officially Faces DC’s God of Death in New Release

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lade Omotade is a News and Features Author at Collider, covering everything from casting announcements and streaming trends to behind-the-scenes stories and the cultural impact of today's biggest film and TV franchises. Since joining the team in 2024, she has written more than 1,000 news stories, with her work also cited by outlets like AOL and IMDb.

Superman is back officially, and this time he's facing one of DC's most powerful supervillains: Nekron. For those unfamiliar, Nekron, also known as the Lord of the Unliving, is an incarnation of Death with seemingly limitless powers. Essentially, he's a deeply foreboding dark force in the DC Universe who draws power from the dead, commands their souls and spirits, and can raise the dead.

This god-like character was created by Mike W. Barr, Len Wein, and Joe Staton primarily as an enemy of the Green Lantern Corps. He first appeared in Tales of the Green Lantern Corps #2, published in June 1981. He also served as the primary antagonist in the 2009–2010 crossover storyline "Blackest Night" and appeared in another crossover, "Wrath of the First Lantern." And of all his appearances, "Blackest Night" is by far the most memorable.

In "Blackest Night," Black Hand summons Nekron to invade the living world, prompting the Lord of the Unliving to resurrect the dead residents of Coast City. He does this to build a massive army for the Black Lantern Corps and psychologically break Hal Jordan. Though Nekron seems evil, he is not motivated by personal hatred, greed, or cruelty. Instead, he is driven by a cosmic urge to restore the universe to its original state of silent, lifeless darkness, since, from his perspective, life and light are the true intruders.

Four teams, four completely different reasons to put yourself in danger for strangers. This is the roster you actually belong on, what they would want from you, and what it would cost you to stay.

You belong where the stakes are largest and the personalities are loudest. This is a team of people who are all used to being the most important person in the room, which is exactly why it works and exactly why it keeps nearly falling apart.

You have never had the luxury of being thanked for this. The X-Men protect a world that has never once been comfortable with them existing, and something in you understands exactly why they keep doing it anyway.

You do not want colleagues, you want your people. The Titans are the team where the friendship came first and the world-saving turned up afterwards as an inconvenient side effect.

You are not asking who to trust. You worked out the answer a while ago and it was nobody in a cape. This is where you go once you have decided the people in charge need watching, and that nobody else is going to do it.

Recently, Nekron's influence and the Black Lantern Corps have resurfaced in Green Lantern #40 and Superman #43, both available from DC Comics. Next, fans will see him return in Superman #44, releasing tomorrow, Wednesday, November 25.

Written by Joshua Williamson, with art and the main cover by Xermánico, Superman #44 follows the title character as he investigates the disappearance of an old friend and several citizens, while also uncovering the truth behind the secret cult in Metropolis introduced in Superman #43. But his search takes a deadly turn when he discovers that the cult worships a corrupted Black Lantern ring. The ring tries to claim him as its new master and force the Man of Steel to trigger Nekron's resurrection.

By Superman #46, releasing January 27, 2027, Superman will face the resurrected Nekron to undo his friend's death, while Marilyn Moonlight and Silver Banshee fight off hordes of Black Lantern zombies in Metropolis. This resurrection ties in with events in Green Lantern #40, where Alexandra DeWitt was recently revived as Nekron's new Black Hand.

DC Comics' Superman #44 launches tomorrow.

Disney Greenlights ‘Aquamarine’ Series Inspired by the 2006 Mermaid Movie; Emma Roberts to Reprise Role and Exec Produce

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kenzi Richardson stars in the live-action series. Along with Roberts, original star Jake McDorman and the film's director Elizabeth Allen Rosenbaum also are on board.

Disney has greenlighted Aquamarine, a new live-action series inspired by the 2006 feature film.

Original stars Emma Roberts and Jake McDorman are reprising their roles from the cult classic and also serving as executive producers. Elizabeth Allen Rosenbaum, who directed the original movie, directs the pilot and exec produces alongside Susan Cartsonis, who produced the film.

In the series, Kenzi Richardson stars as (Coral), a teenager who moves to a dreamy seaside town. There, she begins unraveling the truth behind her mother’s disappearance and discovers her mother is a mermaid, awakening magical powers in Coral just as secrets beneath the waves threaten to surface. Related Stories TV 'Only Murders in the Building' Stars Steve Martin and Martin Short, Without Selena Gomez, Cross Pond for Star-Studded Season 6 London Premiere Beyond the Book 'Percy Jackson and the Olympians': How Season 3 Brings Epic Moments to Life and Deals With Annabeth's Absence in the Books

The cast also includes Briella Guiza (as Mia), Theodore Dalton (as August) and Annika Reese (as Paige). Roberts (who played Claire in the film) and McDorman (Raymond) reprise their roles from the film and serve as executive producers.

“Aquamarine is a timeless coming-of-age story, and we’re excited to bring its humor, heart and sense of wonder to a new generation,” said Ayo Davis, president of Disney Kids & Family. “At its core, it’s a story about the power of friendship, self-discovery and believing in yourself. And with a stellar cast led by Kenzi Richardson, there’s a magical quality to this story that I think audiences everywhere will really connect with.”

The original film, which was loosely based on Alice Hoffman’s 2001 YA novel, followed two teenage girls, played by Roberts and Joanna “JoJo” Levesque, who discover that mermaids really do exist after a violent storm washes one ashore. The mermaid, a sassy creature named Aquamarine (Sara Paxton), is determined to prove to her father that real love exists; she also enlists the girls’ help in winning the heart of a handsome lifeguard (McDorman).

The movie earned moderately positive reviews but was not a huge box office hit when it was released. However, it’s become a cult classic in the years since its release, and its fan base includes some famous faces. Addison Rae has said her song “Aquamarine” was inspired by the film, and Chappell Roan has described her music video for “Casual” as “Aquamarine, but like gay.”

Disney had ordered a pilot for the live-action series over the summer. The show is executive produced by Melissa Carter (The Cleaning Lady), Rosenbaum (Purple Hearts, Guarding Stars) and Cartsonis (Descendants 3), alongside Roberts and McDorman. The pilot is written by Sarah Watson (The Bold Type), directed by Rosenbaum, and executive produced by Watson, Rosenbaum, Cartsonis, Roberts and McDorman.

Disney said the series builds on its expanding slate of coming-of-age shows for Disney+ and Hulu that also includes Percy Jackson and the Olympians, Coven Academy and Phony.

‘Aquamarine’ TV Series Greenlit at Disney+, Disney Channel

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Disney has officially picked up the series adaptation of “Aquamarine,” Variety has learned.

The live-action series will air on both Disney Channel and Disney+. It was originally announced with a pilot order back in June.

The series stars Kenzi Richardson, Briella Guiza, Theodore Dalton, and Annika Reese. Emma Roberts and Jake McDorman, who both starred in the original 2006 film, will reprise their roles in the series. Related Stories Netflix Orders Harlan Coben Limited Series 'The Innocent' From 'I Will Find You' Team Maleah Joi Moon on Netflix’s ‘A Different World,’ Debbie Allen’s Backstage Offer and Owning the Show’s ‘Complicated History’ With Cosby

“‘Aquamarine’ is a timeless coming-of-age story, and we’re excited to bring its humor, heart and sense of wonder to a new generation,” said Ayo Davis, president of Disney Kids & Family. “At its core, it’s a story about the power of friendship, self-discovery and believing in yourself. And with a stellar cast led by Kenzi Richardson, there’s a magical quality to this story that I think audiences everywhere will really connect with.”

The logline for the show states, “When teenager Coral (Richardson) moves to a dreamy seaside town, she begins unraveling the truth behind her mother’s disappearance — and discovers her mother is a mermaid, awakening magical powers in Coral just as secrets beneath the waves threaten to surface.”

The project hails from writer and executive producer Sarah Watson (“The Bold Type”), with the film’s director, Elizabeth Allen Rosenbaum, directing the pilot and executive producing. Susan Cartsonis, who produced the film, also executive produces. Both Roberts and McDorman are executive producers on the series. John Quaintance and Jessica Bendinger wrote the original film, which was based on a novel of the same name by Alice Hoffman. Quintance, Bendinger, and Hoffman are not involved in the series.

Along with Roberts and McDorman, “Aquamarine” starred Joanna “JoJo” Levesque, Sara Paxton, and Arielle Kebbel. In the film, best friends Claire (Roberts) and Hailey (Levesque) try to help mermaid Aquamarine (Paxton) find true love. The film was not particularly well-received when it was first released, but has since achieved cult status.

‘Aquamarine’ Gets Disney Series Greenlight; Emma Roberts & Jake McDorman To Reprise Film Roles & Exec Produce

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

An adaptation of the 2006 film Aquamarine has been picked up to series at Disney+ and Disney Channel, Disney announced Tuesday.

Ordered to pilot in June, Aquamarine stars Kenzi Richardson as Coral, alongside Briella Guiza (Mia), Theodore Dalton (August) and Annika Reese (Paige). Emma Roberts and Jake McDorman reprise their roles as Claire and Raymond, respectively,. from the film, and executive produce.

In the series, when teenager Coral (Richardson) moves to a dreamy seaside town, she begins unraveling the truth behind her mother’s disappearance — and discovers her mother is a mermaid, awakening magical powers in Coral just as secrets beneath the waves threaten to surface. Related Stories News 'Percy Jackson': How A Quest To Save Annabeth Makes Everyone Reevaluate Their Relationships In Season 3 Casting 'Afterlife With Archie' Disney+ Series Casts Its Archie, Veronica, Betty, Jughead & Sabrina

“Aquamarine is a timeless coming-of-age story, and we’re excited to bring its humor, heart and sense of wonder to a new generation,” said Ayo Davis, president, Disney Kids & Family. “At its core, it’s a story about the power of friendship, self-discovery and believing in yourself. And with a stellar cast led by Kenzi Richardson, there’s a magical quality to this story that I think audiences everywhere will really connect with.” Watch on Deadline

Elizabeth Allen Rosenbaum (Purple Hearts), who directed the original film, directs the pilot and executive produces alongside Susan Cartsonis (Descendants 3), who produced the original film. The Bold Type creator Sarah Watson penned the pilot and executive produces alongside Melissa Carter (The Cleaning Lady), Roberts and McDorman.

The 2006 movie is loosely based on Alice Hoffman’s 2001 YA novel. It starred Roberts as Claire, along with Joanna “JoJo” Levesque and Sara Paxton. In it, teenage best friends, Claire and Hailey (Levesque), discover a sassy mermaid named Aquamarine washed ashore after a storm. Fleeing an arranged marriage, she promises to grant them a wish if they can prove to her that true love exists by winning the heart of a local lifeguard. Since its release, the film has reached cult status.

Aquamarine joins supernatural dramedy Coven Academy, which was picked up to series at Disney-owned Hulu following a pilot order in 2025, as well as Nick Paley’s coming-of-age mystery Phony, and Durango, starring Cooper Hoffman and Kathryn Newton, both of which had received previous pilot orders. Ryan Coogler’s reimagining of The X-Files was picked up to series at Hulu in October following a pilot order earlier this year.

Planes, Trains And Automobiles' Deleted Ending Was Rightfully Cut

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Planes, Trains and Automobiles was originally supposed to have a completely different ending, but thankfully it was removed before release. Few comedy movies have endings quite as emotionally satisfying as Planes, Trains and Automobiles. John Hughes' 1987 road-trip comedy spends most of its running time putting advertising executive Neal Page (Steve Martin) through an increasingly ridiculous series of travel disasters, largely thanks to his unwanted traveling companion, Del Griffith (John Candy). However, beneath the hilarious arguments, unfortunate misunderstandings and spectacular misfortunes is a surprisingly moving story about loneliness, friendship and the importance of appreciating other people.

Planes, Trains and Automobiles’ final moments bring that story together beautifully. After finally reaching Chicago, Neal initially leaves Del behind, only to realize that his traveling companion has nowhere to go. He returns to the train station, discovers the truth about Del's circumstances, and invites him home for Thanksgiving. It's a wonderfully understated conclusion that allows both characters to experience a genuine emotional breakthrough.

However, this wasn't always how Hughes intended to finish the movie. The original Planes, Trains and Automobiles screenplay featured a considerably longer ending, including a more explicit explanation of Del's tragic circumstances and an extended Thanksgiving dinner with Neal's family. Although the additional material offered some interesting character details, Hughes ultimately made the right decision by cutting it.

In the original Planes, Trains and Automobiles screenplay, Neal initially leaves Del behind after they finally reach Chicago. When he boards the train home, Del somehow turns up at his destination, prompting Neal to question why he is still following him. Del eventually explains that his wife, Marie, died eight years earlier and that he has been living out of his trunk, moving between motels and occasionally attaching himself to strangers for companionship (via Den of Geek).

The screenplay included a much longer explanation of Del's circumstances, detailing his wife's illness, their inability to have children, and his increasing difficulty coping with life alone. He also explains that Thanksgiving is particularly painful because it reminds him of the family he never had. Rather than Neal figuring out the truth, this version has Del explicitly explain his loneliness and why he attaches himself to other people.

The original ending then continued to Neal's family home, where Del joined the Thanksgiving celebrations. The screenplay included an extended dinner sequence, with Del expressing his gratitude for being welcomed into a family gathering. Audiences at test screenings reportedly did not respond well to the ending, finding Del’s monolog frustrating and unintentionally funny, prompting the change.

The change to the Planes, Trains and Automobiles ending gives Del considerably more dignity. Throughout Planes, Trains and Automobiles, his relentless friendliness is frequently presented as a source of frustration for Neal. He's loud, intrusive, and seemingly incapable of recognizing when somebody wants to be left alone. However, the final revelation reframes his behavior without suggesting that his personality is simply a disguise for his loneliness.

In the original screenplay, Del's explanation risks making his invitation to Thanksgiving feel like an act of charity. Neal discovers the truth because Del effectively has to ask for help, and their reconciliation becomes dependent on an extended confession. Although the material provides valuable background, it also removes some of the emotional complexity from their relationship. Del becomes somebody to pity rather than somebody Neal has genuinely learned to appreciate.

The theatrical version is much more thoughtful. Neal doesn't need Del to explain his situation because he has finally started paying attention to him. Audiences don't need to see Del sitting around the table, meeting every member of Neal's family or explaining how grateful he is to have somewhere to spend the holiday. The simple image of the two men walking together with Del's enormous trunk before settling on Del's earnest smile communicates everything necessary,

Planes, Trains and Automobiles is at its best when its comedy and sentiment exist alongside one another rather than competing for attention. The theatrical ending preserves that balance, allowing the audience to experience the same realization as Neal. Hughes understood that the most affecting way to conclude Del and Neal's journey throughout Planes, Trains and Automobiles wasn't to explain their newfound friendship, but to show that it had finally happened.

Thank you for subscribing to our newsletter,

Stephen King's 6 Non-Horror Shows, Ranked Worst To Best

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Stephen King’s name is synonymous with horror, but he’s written in multiple genres over the decades. From sci-fi thrillers like The Dead Zone to fantasy series like The Dark Tower, King's books are incredibly varied. While it might seem like every TV adaptation of King’s novels involves horror (to be fair, most do), there have actually been some adaptations of his non-horror works over the years. However, as with most Stephen King adaptations, the quality varies across the board.

Some of these non-horror adaptations are mostly faithful to the source material, while others are so different from the original work, they have almost nothing in common. A few are well-written, incredibly compelling, and look fantastic. Then, there are the others that might be entertaining, but suffer from writing and pacing issues. A few Stephen King non-horror works still haven’t been adapted or used to their full potential, so while this handful of shows is all that’s available now, hopefully, more adaptations will be made in the future.

Golden Years is likely a show few remember, as it aired only seven episodes on CBS in 1991. For those wondering when they missed the Stephen King novel of the same name — there wasn’t one. This adaptation actually came after King had written down the idea for it in a notebook, where it sat for years. He eventually decided to write it as a “novel for television,” according to an interview in The New York Times, which resulted in this miniseries.

While Golden Years was designed as a lead-in to a regular series, CBS opted not to pick it up. As a result, the TV show ended on a cliffhanger (though the home video version changed a few minutes of the last episode to give it a more hopeful ending). The series followed Harlan Williams, an elderly janitor who is exposed to chemicals after an explosion in a secret lab.

As a result, he begins aging in reverse, which gets the attention of those in charge of the lab. Soon, Harlan and his wife have to go on the run, where they meet Terry Spann, who has decided to help protect them. Of course, Harlan’s sudden youthful tendencies aren’t the only powers to develop; as time goes on, other strange things begin to happen. Starring Keith Szarabajka, Frances Sternhagen, R.D. Call, and Felicity Huffman, Golden Years is a decent little sci-fi thriller that mostly suffers from pacing issues and underdeveloped plot elements (and a lack of conclusion).

The second-longest-running Stephen King adaptation, Haven, is based on King’s The Colorado Kid only in the loosest sense. While the novel is a crime mystery involving the mysterious death of a stranger and a pair of older journalists sharing his tale with a newer, much younger journalist, Haven basically borrows only a few names, the location, and minor elements from the novel. The show still involves a dead man nicknamed The Colorado Kid, but it’s more of a procedural with supernatural elements.

Some Stephen King TV shows have fallen under the radar, among them a five-season sci-fi thriller that all fans of the King of Horror should watch.

When FBI Agent Audrey Parker arrives in the small town of Haven on a routine case, she soon discovers more is going on beneath its surface. The people of the town are afflicted with what they call The Troubles. These supernatural afflictions range from having what one draws come true to being a human bullet magnet. She soon quits the FBI and joins the local police, helping those who are Troubled, while also trying to figure out the mystery of her past.

It’s kind of like The X-Files but with a lower budget and less charm, and eventually the lore gets amazingly convoluted. However, Emily Rose as Park is compelling, while Eric Balfour as Duke Crocker is a blast. Best of all, Edge (the wrestler who used to tag team with Christian in the early aughts) is in the show and does a surprisingly good job.

Under the Dome is another adaptation that varies considerably from its source material. However, in this case, Stephen King took to his website during its first season to reassure fans upset about the differences and to say he wholeheartedly approved of the changes thus far. He also pointed out that the novel covers eight days, while the TV show covers a far longer period, so some changes were necessary. Those changes worked, too, as the first season of the series was intriguing.

The series follows the town of Chester’s Mill, where, as one might guess, an indestructible, transparent dome appears one day, cutting the town off from the outside world. While the military and others outside try to figure out how to get rid of the dome, those inside are left to survive dwindling resources and the inevitability of people turning on one another as fear takes hold.

However, fans and critics both soured on the series when it returned for its second and third season. Even King himself eventually decided he didn't like Under the Dome's small-screen adaptation, having initially been a fan. Unfortunately, the idea of a town stuck under a dome, told over multiple seasons, was spreading the premise a bit too thin. Under the Dome was reasonably decent as standard sci-fi fare, but it also leaned into its ridiculous elements a bit too much. Add in characters making increasingly irrational decisions, and things went far off course.

The longest-running King adaptation was The Dead Zone, which ran for six seasons. A seventh season was expected, but unfortunately, the series was canceled, partially due to financial concerns. That left the series hanging on a cliffhanger that answered some questions but raised many more. While it doesn’t have a Rotten Tomatoes score, it does carry a 7.3 on IMDb.

Stephen King's underrated six-season show is now available to stream for free, promising a blend of genres to appeal to different viewers.

Like the 1979 novel and the 1983 movie, the TV series follows Johnny Smith, a teacher who falls into a coma after a car accident and wakes up five years later. When he wakes, he finds himself having visions of the future and the past when he touches people and things. Like the novel, Johnny also discovers through a vision that politician Greg Stillson will kick off the apocalypse in the future. Unlike the novel, the show is much more of a procedural as Johnny begins solving crimes with the help of his abilities.

Overall, the sci-fi drama was enjoyable, with pretty decent visual effects depicting Johnny’s visions, even if it does veer away from the novel due to its long run. However, along with lacking a proper conclusion, The Dead Zone sometimes relied on overly formulaic storylines andoccasional shallow writing.

King explored the age-old question of what a person would do if given the chance to go back in time and prevent a tragedy in his sci-fi novel 11.22.63, when teacher Jake Epping goes back in time to stop the assassination of John F. Kennedy by Lee Harvey Oswald. The miniseries adaptation does the same, though with a few changes to the original storyline. For instance, George MacKay plays the character Bill Turcotte in 11.22.63's TV iteration, someone who doesn't exist in the book.

As Epping undertakes his mission, time seemingly begins pushing back through accidents that crop up and get in the way of his goal. He also falls in love in the past and tries to prevent smaller events while he’s there. However, as with all sci-fi time travel, the smallest of changes can have fairly dire consequences in the future.

It: Welcome to Derry is easily one of the best takes on Stephen King’s works, but a handful of other TV adaptations of his books are arguably better.

This King adaptation is well-made, and the design is incredibly authentic to the era it’s set in (not surprising, given how much research King did to make the novel historically authentic). Some fans weren’t keen on the changes to the novel’s plot, but overall, it’s a thought-provoking, bingeworthy eight episodes. The pacing does tend to drag a bit in the middle, but the series' emotional depth and strong performances save it from cratering.

An adaptation of King’s as yet unbeatable Bill Hodges trilogy, which includes the books Mr. Mercedes, Finders Keepers, and End of Watch, Mr. Mercedes was one of the best TV shows based on King’s works. The series stays far more faithful to the novels than most adaptations, and focuses on Bill Hodges, a retired detective who is still haunted by one particular case where someone drove a stolen Mercedes into a group of people at a local job fair, killing 16.

David E. Kelley (The Practice, Big Little Lies) developed the series, so it was led by someone who knew what they were doing when it came to crime thrillers. The first season pits Bill Hodges against the young psychopath Brady Hartsfield, and soon the two are drawn into a serious game of cat-and-mouse. This season is instantly intriguing, full of high tension, rich in suspense, and complex characters. The performances are outstanding, particularly by Brendan Gleeson as Bill and Harry Treadaway as Brady.

This hard-boiled detective thriller can be bleak at times, and though it isn’t horror, the subject matter can still feel creepy. While the other two seasons were decent, the quality declined a bit, and some fans feel the series should have ended after the first season. Mr. Mercedes suffered from pacing issues as time went on, as well as inconsistent writing that did it no favors. Still, if one only wants to watch a single Stephen King non-horror adaptation, this is probably the best bet.

Focker-In-Law Review: A Few Good Laughs In A Film That Falls Flat

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ben Stiller's 26-year-old franchise is back with a new sequel, and while it has the heart of the original at times, it ultimately misses the mark.

Ben Stiller's Meet the Parents franchise began in 2000, and fueled by the on-screen chemistry of Stiller and Robert De Niro, along with a host of brutally awkward scenes, it quickly found popularity. Ultimately, Meet the Parents was seen as a cult classic, and it was followed by two sequels, Meet the Fockers in 2004 and Little Fockers in 2010.

Unfortunately, neither Meet the Fockers nor Little Fockers found the success of their predecessor. It is against that backdrop that Stiller, somewhat surprisingly, decided to take another stab at it 16 years later with the brand-new fourth installment, Focker-in-Law.

Focker-in-Law sees the return of the core cast members, with Stiller's Greg and De Niro's Jack still taking center stage. However, the franchise has added some star power to the next generation, with the DCU's Skyler Gisondo playing Greg's son Henry and singer and actress Ariana Grande playing his fiancé, Olivia. Perhaps, with that beefed-up cast, Universal was hoping for a repeat of the original that side-stepped the two follow-up flops. Sadly, Focker-in-Law offers much of the same.

In truth, Focker-in-Law got several genuine laughs out of me. It's impossible to deny how naturally funny Stiller is, and his dynamic with De Niro clearly works, given the legacy of the franchise. Yet, for as much as Greg's antics occasionally find their original charm, they also raise questions about whether this concept is well and truly played out. In fact, in a moment that is played for laughs, the movie points out that Greg is now older than Jack was when they first met.

Rather than reading as funny, this revelation underscores the fact that Greg has evidently never matured, nor has he really grown as a person. If anything, he is all the worse in this movie. Whereas in Meet the Parents in particular, he is a well-meaning, nervous future son-in-law who accidentally creates mayhem wherever he goes, some of Greg's behavior in Focker-in-Law is outright mean, and borders on cruel.

Unfortunately, the addition of Grande's Olivia fails to help. Ahead of the movie's release, there were grumbles about Grande joining the cast; however, she had plenty of potential to shine in the role. Her appearance in projects like Wicked have proven that she can, indeed, act (and she actually started out acting), and many of her Saturday Night Live skits have been incredibly funny, suggesting that she was up to the task of a comedy.

Yet, in Focker-in-Law, Grande's charm and wit are largely absent. Hampered by a script that doesn't seem to know what it wants to do with this new character, most of Olivia's laugh lines fall flat, and while the Meet the Parents franchise has always stretched the limits of realism, Olivia's skills, talents, and apparent ability to succeed in every single area of her life border on the absurd. In fairness, this has less to do with Grande and more to do with Focker-in-Law's refusal to give her nuance – or, frankly, any realistic, humanizing flaws.

Gisondo, meanwhile, is a standout among the cast. Arguably, he out-acts his co-stars more often than not, and he consistently seems to be the only person on screen committed to conveying a mood other than silly or goofy. That may very well be because most characters were not granted the room to explore other emotions. Teri Polo's Pam and Blythe Danner's Dina are all but sidelined, with the latter in particular being almost non-existent.

Still, Focker-in-Law isn't a complete flop. Those looking for a few good laughs and a nostalgia-packed return of the franchise will no doubt enjoy the movie, and even those who are just hoping for a feel-good film for the holiday season will have plenty of fun. It's far from the most inventive movie of 2026, to be sure, but it shouldn't be counted out altogether.

Focker-in-Law releases in theaters nationwide on Wednesday, November 25.

The Fate of ‘The Rings of Power’ Has Officially Been Decided

Right now is an exciting time to be a Lord of the Rings fan, even if it’s going to be over a year before the franchise returns to the big screen. Returning to star in the new movie coming to theaters on December 17, 2027, are Lee Pace as Thranduil, Elijah Wood as Frodo Baggins, and even Ian McKellen as Gandalf, but Viggo Mortensen has been replaced by Jamie Dornan as Aragorn in the new film. News broke this afternoon that The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power has been officially renewed for Season 4. This news dropping mere hours before the final two Season 4 episodes premiere on Prime Video should help convince some fans who are on the fence about watching it, especially those who were worried the show was going to be canceled. We also have an idea of when Season 4 will be released.

This extra progress will only help get things back on track quicker.

When the Cardinals hired Mike LaFleur in February, he was the last head coach to start his job in this year’s NFL cycle.

That had consequences for his first group of assistants.

According to Josh Weinfuss of ESPN, LaFleur initially wanted to move on from defensive coordinator Nick Rallis. But being the last one hired, LaFleur had limited options for a new DC and elected to retain Rallis.

Teryl Austin will take over as interim defensive coordinator. LaFleur noted he’s focused on the present when it comes to Austin becoming a candidate for the full-time role.

4 reasons to grab the Disney+ and Hulu bundle deal, from $4.99 a month

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Disney+ and Hulu bundle starts at $4.99 a month for 12 months. See 4 reasons to take the deal and the catch attached to each one.

Mashable laid out why the Disney+ and Hulu Black Friday bundle offer is worth buying, with reasons that include an ad-free option and a bonus Disney gift card. Each of those reasons comes with a condition attached. Some of the savings require sitting through commercials, some hold for only a set period, and one depends on a library that Disney is still changing.

Viewers who want this price have to decide quickly. Disney accepts sign-ups at the discounted rate only from Nov. 24 until 11:59 p.m. PT on Nov. 30, and a viewer who waits until December pays the regular rate.

Other streamers are cutting prices this week too, and how long each low price lasts matters more than any single month's bill. Apple $AAPL charges $6.99 a month for Apple TV+ on its Black Friday deal, and only for six months, according to 9to5Google. The Disney+ and Hulu bundle costs less each month and holds its rate for a full year. The length of that term is the best reason to take this deal.

For a viewer who can live with ads and signs up in time, this is the cheapest way into both libraries for the coming year, as long as that viewer budgets for the regular price once the year is over.

For a viewer who would pay to avoid ads, this deal makes avoiding them cheaper.

Disney is selling the merger as more to watch in one place, not as two libraries squeezed together. The unified Disney+ will include "live TV and select premium add-ons," Disney CEO Josh D'Amaro and CFO Hugh Johnston said in their quarterly shareholder letter, as reported by Variety. That promise reaches beyond two film and TV catalogs. The word "select" promises only some extra services, and a viewer who wants a particular add-on should confirm it is offered before counting on it.

This reason holds up: the second library is real, and buyers should expect to find it inside Disney+.

For someone who would check the Tuesday perks anyway, the gift card is a modest bonus, but it is no reason on its own to choose one bundle over another.

‘The Terminal List’ Star’s $133M War Thriller Is Leaving Netflix

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lade Omotade is a News and Features Author at Collider, covering everything from casting announcements and streaming trends to behind-the-scenes stories and the cultural impact of today's biggest film and TV franchises. Since joining the team in 2024, she has written more than 1,000 news stories, with her work also cited by outlets like AOL and IMDb.

About a year ago, Kathryn Bigelow returned to the spotlight with her acclaimed political thriller, A House of Dynamite. Released on Netflix in October 2025, the "masterfully constructed" war film explored how U.S. government officials react to a missile attack. It quickly became a critics' favorite upon release, earning praise for its performances and Bigelow's precise direction; qualities that may prompt many to recall her similarly gripping political thriller from over a decade ago.

In January 2013, Bigelow's Oscar-winning drama opened in theaters following its 2012 premiere. The film was hugely successful, earning a Certified Fresh rating on Rotten Tomatoes, where it now boasts a 91% score. Unsurprisingly, praise focused on Bigelow's brilliant direction as well as lead star Jessica Chastain's magnetic performance. The action thriller was also a box office success, topping the wide-release premiere weekend and grossing more than $132 million worldwide: $95.7 million in the U.S. and Canada and $37.1 million in other countries.

Since its launch, Zero Dark Thirty has found renewed success through home media and streaming. Now, 13 years later, it's saying goodbye to Netflix, officially leaving the streamer on December 1, 2026. That gives you only six days to catch up before it's removed. Along with directing the film, Bigelow produced it with Megan Ellison and Mark Boal, who also wrote it.

Joel Edgerton listening to someone in a military outpost in Zero Dark Thirty

Joel Edgerton listening to someone in a military outpost in Zero Dark Thirty

Starring Chastain as Maya, a CIA intelligence analyst, Zero Dark Thirty follows the decade-long international hunt for Osama bin Laden after the 9/11 terrorist attacks. The story is told from the perspective of the American operatives involved, with Maya becoming increasingly consumed by the mission.

Jason Clarke and Joel Edgerton appear in supporting roles as CIA intelligence officer Dan Fuller and DEVGRU (SEAL Team 6) team leader Patrick Grayston, respectively. The Terminal List favorite Chris Pratt also appears as DEVGRU operator Justin Lenihan, who was involved in the hunt. Other stars featured in Zero Dark Thirty are Jennifer Ehle, Mark Strong, Kyle Chandler, James Gandolfini, Harold Perrineau, Mark Duplass, and Jeremy Strong.

Although Zero Dark Thirty received a phenomenal response and earned multiple accolades, it also sparked controversy over its portrayal of torture, CIA access, and historical accuracy. In 2013, Al Jazeera described the first third of the film as "unadulterated torture porn," with the CIA at the center, while U.S. senators John McCain, Dianne Feinstein, and Carl Levin accused it of being "pro-torture."

Zero Dark Thirty leaves Netflix in less than a week.

‘The Lord Of The Rings: The Rings Of Power’ Renewed For Season 4 At Amazon

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Amazon is showing faith in Middle Earth after renewing The Lord of The Rings: The Rings of Power for a fourth season.

The streamer has confirmed that the fantasy drama is in pre-production for a fourth run and will begin filming at Shepperton Studios in the UK in the spring.

It comes as Amazon prepares to launch the final two episodes of season three at 6pm PT today (November 24).

J.D. Payne and Patrick McKay, who serve as showrunners and exec producers of the series, will return. The pair have long said that they have a five-season plan for The Rings of Power and are hoping to stick with it. This comes despite some criticism from fans and the fact that ratings haven’t quite been what Amazon would have wanted, having spent so much money translating J.R.R Tolkien’s work to the small screen.

Ratings for the show have dropped since season one; its second season saw a steep drop off in viewers and the streamer hasn’t revealed any numbers for season three, although the show failed to hit Luminate’s streaming top ten after it launched. Watch on Deadline

RELATED: Amazon MGM Talks $1B Slate And Says “We Are Heavily Invested In Theatrical” & “We’re Not Holding Any Content Back”

The third season features a time jump, taking place seven years after the events of season two. It begins at the height of the War of the Elves and Sauron (Charlie Vickers). It depicts the Dark Lord’s efforts to create the One Ring, the first time that the forging of the iconic ring is ever shown on screen.

The renewal marks the first one for Peter Friedlander, Head of Global Television, Amazon MGM Studios, who praised the showrunners.

“The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power has become a defining series for Prime Video,” he said Peter Friedlander. “As Season Three delivers the landmark moment fans have been waiting for — the forging of the One Ring — we are proud to confirm Season Four with J.D. Payne, Patrick McKay, and the entire creative team. We look forward to bringing fans even deeper into the rich world of Tolkien’s Second Age.”

It stars Vickers, Jamie Campbell Bower, Morfydd Clark, Ismael Cruz Cordova, Tyroe Muhafidin, Robert Aramayo, Owain Arthur, Sophia Nomvete, Benjamin Walker, Daniel Weyman, Trystan Gravelle, Maxim Baldry, Cynthia Addai-Robinson, Lloyd Owen, Ciaran Hinds, Andrew Richardson and Rory Kinnear.

Payne and McKay exec produce alongside Lindsey Weber, Kate Hazell, Justin Doble and Charlotte Brändström, who also directs. Matthew Penry-Davey is producer and Ally O’Leary Cantrell, Tim Keene, and Andrew Lee are co-producers.

Guy Pearce's Gory '90s Horror Western Movie Deserved Better At The Box Office

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

One of the enduring American monster myths is the Wendigo or the forest spirit that possesses and compels men to become cannibals. Originally a myth of the Algonquian peoples, the Wendigo has become a monster of comic books, horror films, and more, like Stephen King's chilling novel "Pet Sematary."

Possibly the strangest Wendigo movie is the 1999 horror western comedy "Ravenous," inspired by real tales of 19th century cannibalism on the frontier like the Donner Party. During the Mexican–American War, Lieutenant John Boyd (Guy Pearce) plays dead during battle. Hidden among a cart of deceased soldiers, Boyd takes an enemy fort. A hero but also a coward, Boyd is then reassigned to a remote outpost in the Sierra Nevada mountains.

Curiously, during Boyd's seizure of the fort, he seemed to gain strength when the blood of the dead dripped down onto his tongue. In that moment, Boyd became an unknowing Wendigo, acquiring the strength of the men whose blood he drank — and he's not the only one on the mountain. A traveler named F.W. Colqhoun (Robert Carlyle) shows up at the fort, claiming to be the survivor of a band of wagon travelers killed and cannibalized by their guide, one Colonel Ives. But "Colqhoun" is Ives and, seizing control of the camp, plots to turn it into a cannibal colony. He recognizes Boyd as a seeming kindred spirit, and the movie becomes a test of whether Boyd will succumb to Ives' pressure and his own Wendigo hunger.

"Ravenous" was a box office bomb in its day, grossing $2 million against a $12 million budget, and it received largely underwhelmed or confounded reviews. But don't take that as a mark against the movie, which has a following to this day. (Any "Yellowjackets" fan should make watching "Ravenous" a priority.)

For "Ravenous" to work, Boyd's temptation needs to feel real. When you've got someone as charismatic as Robert Carlyle playing Ives, it sure does. That performance isn't the only way that Carlyle saved the movie.

"Ravenous" had a tumultuous production, and Guy Pearce has called "Ravenous" his worst experience shooting a movie. The film's original director Milcho Manchevski was fired mid-filming with 20th Century Fox moving to replace him with "Home Alone 3" director Raja Gosnell. The cast rejected that, and so Carlyle brought in director Antonia Bird, who he'd worked with on the 1997 crime film "Face." Bird is the credited director of "Ravenous," but was she able to wrangle the mess she'd inherited?

Even a "Ravenous" fan will admit the movie is, tonally, all over the place. After a scene of Ives revealing his true murderous self, the movie breaks out into a chase scene with comical banjo music. If you'll pardon the pun, "Ravenous" bites off a lot and tests how much it's capable of chewing. But that strangeness is a strength, as there's no other movie quite like "Ravenous."

In a 2015 retrospective review for Rolling Stone, critic David Ehrlich showed the kind of praise that has earned "Ravenous" a fanbase. Ehrlich complimented its unique tonal recipe and how, through its setting, it equates manifest destiny colonialism with literal cannibalism... while conceding those qualities are likely what turned off audiences in 1999.

Considering the 1990s saw Anthony Hopkins make Hannibal Lecter into a cinematic icon on the heels "The Silence of the Lambs," I can see why studio execs would invest in cannibal horror. But "Ravenous" was just too weird a movie to ever be a massive hit, even though it's found its supporters with time.

GTA 6 launch knocked Call of Duty's daily players down 42 percent, Alinea estimates

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6's launch took a big bite out of Call of Duty’s player base, according to new estimates from Alinea Analytics, and four days on, it still hasn't recovered.

Writing in Alinea's newsletter, Rhys Elliott said Call of Duty HQ's daily active users (DAUs) across PlayStation, Xbox, and Steam fell 42 percent when GTA 6 launched on 19th November, dropping from an average of 7.5 million to 4.4 million. On PlayStation, which Elliott describes as "CoD's main platform", the drop was 44 percent.

"Sadly, CoD has barely bounced back after four days," Elliott wrote. "It's still down 41% from its baseline."

Fortnite and FC 27 took a hit too, but both are already recovering. Fortnite's DAUs on PlayStation and Xbox fell 28 percent, from 17.8 million to 12.8 million, but by the end of the weekend it had clawed its way back to 19 percent below its baseline.

Epic is also holding a live event to close out the current season on 5th December, which Elliott says the studio "hopes will pull players back after they’ve polished off GTA 6’s story".

FC 27's DAUs dropped by around 27 percent, from 5.9 million to 4.3 million, and it's already back within 20 percent of its baseline.

GTA 6's launch hit Call of Duty HARD (@alineaanalytics estimates).COD HQ's daily active users across PlayStation, Xbox, and Steam are down 42% from an average of 7.5M DAUs to 4.4M. On PlayStation – CoD’s main platform – Call of Duty took a 44% hit. Sadly, CoD has barely bounced… pic.twitter.com/XLa6N9TBfr

According to Alinea, of those who'd played GTA 6 by the end of its launch weekend, 29 percent had also played Fortnite this month, 16 percent had played Call of Duty HQ, and 11 percent had played FC 27.

Elliott said the pattern is largely what Alinea had predicted: the big live service games took an instant hit from GTA, but bounced back "because they're ingrained habits". Call of Duty is "the outlier here", he added, "and Xbox should - candidly - be worried about one of its premier franchises".

Elliott also points out that all of this is the impact of GTA 6's single-player mode. He expects its online component and PC port to arrive "some time next year", and when they do, he says, "the bounce back will not be so obtainable".

Alinea also estimates that GTA 6 has sold 44.9 million copies and made more than $4 billion since launch.

GTA 6 launched on Thursday, 19th November, for PS5 and Xbox Series X/S. While it's the biggest entertainment launch of all time, selling 32.5 million pre-release copies and making $3.79 billion by the end of its first day, you wouldn't know it from its Metacritic user score, as players review bomb it over its limited FPS and purported "woke" agenda.

Eurogamer called GTA 6 an open world of "awe, minute detail and impossible scale" in our five-star GTA 6 review. If you need help getting through the game now that it's here, you can check out Eurogamer's Grand Theft Auto 6 walkthrough and a breakdown of our Grand Theft Auto 6 cheats.

Miley Cyrus’ Wedding Venue Was Frank Sinatra’s Former Palm Springs Home—and It’s Available as a Vacation Rental

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After a stunning ceremony in Palm Springs, CA, Miley Cyrus and Maxx Morando are officially husband and wife—and part of the history of an iconic midcentury modern estate that formerly belonged to Frank Sinatra.

The musicians said “I do” on Nov. 21, surrounded by family, friends, and a bounty of stunning flowers. The picturesque celebration took place in the backyard of Twin Palms, right on the edge of the piano-shaped pool the “Fly Me to the Moon” singer once lounged by, according to Elle Decor.

Commissioned by Sinatra in 1947, the 4,500-square-foot home is located in the Movie Colony, a legendary Palm Springs neighborhood that Hollywood entertainers flocked to in the 1930s and 1940s.

The official Twin Palms website explains that Sinatra came to the desert town in hopes of finding a weekend retreat for his family after signing a movie contract with MGM Studios. He was initially looking for a Georgian-style estate with a lavish brick facade, but architect E. Stewart Williams persuaded him to go for something more “desert appropriate.”

The website also claims that the $150,000 commission was a hassle, with Sinatra giving the team just a few months to design and build the property so it would be ready in time for a Christmas party. The abbreviated timeline required contractors to work around the clock and made the process much more expensive.

The six years the famed crooner resided in the home were action-packed. He initially moved in with his first wife, Nancy Barbato, and when their relationship ended in 1948, it would become the backdrop for his highly publicized relationship with second wife, Ava Gardner.

Today, Twin Palms is available as a short-term rental for a variety of needs. It can be used as a vacation home or private event venue, and can also accommodate commercial uses such as photo shoots and filming. While exact pricing varies for vacation rentals, it’s typically about $4,400 a night.

Cyrus and Morando are far from the first couple to tie the knot at Twin Palms, with the website including a gallery of photos from previous weddings. The property can host up to 150 guests and includes a variety of spaces that can be used throughout the evening.

While the “Black Mirror” star opted to seat guests around the pool, the impressive great room, circular motor court, and additional lawn space opposite the pool are also available.

With four bedrooms and seven bathrooms, the estate comfortably sleeps eight guests. Each of the bedrooms boasts an en suite bathroom.

The primary suite has its own wing—a feature that was particularly appealing to Sinatra, who valued privacy.

In true midcentury modern fashion, the home features massive sliding glass doors that integrate indoor and outdoor living, connecting the great room to the sprawling patio and uniquely shaped swimming pool.

A detached pool house includes a kitchenette, bar, and cabana-style bathrooms.

In recent years, the home has been totally restored, using vintage designer furnishings to maintain the original retro character. Twin Palms still has the original sound and recording system, as well as Sinatra’s original recording studio—although, per the website, it is no longer functional.

The kitchen has been updated with modern Viking appliances and St. Charles modern cabinetry to call back to the original design.

In addition to memorabilia celebrating the singer around the home, there’s a chip in a bathroom sink which Sinatra had apparently damaged with a Champagne bottle.

Sinatra reportedly sold the home in 1953, after his split from Gardner. The home was designated a Palm Springs Class 1 Historic Site in 2011, by the Palm Springs Preservation Foundation.

Hannah Chambers is a senior celebrity and lifestyle reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2026. She has been writing entertainment and lifestyle content since 2016, with her work appearing in Cosmopolitan, BravoTV, NYLON, Bustle, Elite Daily, and The Zoe Report, among other publications. Hannah received a bachelor’s degree in journalism from The University of Maine. In her spare time, she loves to chat about pop culture and is a major fan of reality TV.

Avengers: Doomsday Star Teases Ominous Fate for His Character

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Time might be running out for a certain hero

With Avengers: Doomsday a little less than a month away, Marvel shared its third and final trailer for the movie on Monday. The trailer didn't have too many surprises and mostly covered what fans had already seen in previous sneak peeks. As such, longtime Marvel viewers still have plenty of questions about the movie. Top of mind: Who's going to make it out alive?

As the title implies, Doomsday will be a destructive film as universes start colliding in what are called Incursions. Fans expect at least some characters to die and not make it to Phase... whatever is next. (Who can keep track anymore?) In a recent interview, one of the film's stars has hinted that his character may not be long for the Marvel Cinematic Universe.

[Ed. note: Possible spoilers for Avengers: Doomsday follow, depending on how much you want to read into an actor's comments.]

Sebastian Stan has been playing Bucky Barnes/The Winter Soldier since 2011's Captain America: The First Avenger. His character has gone on quite a journey, from being a World War II sergeant to a brainwashed assassin to a Congressman. Stan says he is "always happy" to keep suiting up as the hero, as he said on the Next Best Picture podcast recently, adding, "I don't know if the decision's up to me, ultimately."

While Stan said "we've done our best so far" in exploring Bucky's character, he does think there are more layers to peel back. "I don't think that that character totally got to fully live out his arc in the way that he has in the comics, for instance, or that I've wanted," Stan said. In the comics, Bucky becomes Captain America for a time after Steve Rogers' death, which is what Stan may have been referring to here. On the Happy Sad Confused podcast last month, Stan spoke about wanting to do a lost years project for Bucky and his desire to play Bucky as Captain America while he can still do action sequences. ("Sorry, Anthony," he said, referring to current Captain America actor Anthony Mackie.)

"I'm always ready, but we're at that point now where I can feel it: He needs to either evolve somewhere, or maybe it's time. It's one of those," Stan said. That "maybe it's time" sure sounds like he's saying "maybe it's time" that Bucky dies. Marvel hasn't been shy about killing off characters before, as Natasha Romanoff/Black Widow and Tony Stark/Iron Man both died in Avengers: Endgame. And, of course, who could forget all those heroes getting dusted at the end of Infinity War?

In a separate interview with Radio Times, Stan said he's "just enjoying the ride" and that he's been "lucky" to play Bucky for so long. "It’s bizarre to return to something with all of these people that I’ve known and grown up with for the last 10 to 15 years… it's nostalgic. It’s almost like a college reunion. It’s going to be a very celebratory situation," he said.

Stan's frequent scene partner Chris Evans previously said that directors Anthony and Joe Russo "love beating up" his character Steve Rogers. "They keep finding new ways to kick his butt, but [Doomsday] was brutal, and a really, really good examination into his nature and his values. I'll say, it's probably something you haven't seen yet, but you will," Evans said. What would be a more brutal way to beat Steve Rogers up than by killing his lifelong BFF?

We'll see what fate has in store for Bucky, Steve, and all your other favorite MCU characters when Avengers: Doomsday hits theaters Dec. 18.

Mariah Carey Claps Back at Online Criticism After Recent Performances

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Mariah Carey appeared to address online speculation about her health during a performance in Rio de Janeiro on Nov. 21.

All Mariah Carey wants for Christmas, is to put this topic to rest.

As the queen of the holiday season performed at Farmasi Arena in Rio de Janeiro Nov. 21, she appeared to address speculation surrounding her health following her latest concerts.

“By the way, don’t believe everything you read online,” Carey said in a video shared by fans on social media after the show. “Don’t be making me mad, now.”

The “We Belong Together” singer’s comments shortly followed fan speculation that she was going through some sort of health battle due to the blocking in her recent shows.

“Is Mariah Carey choosing to perform as stiff as possible, or is something going on with her health?” a fan wrote on X earlier this month. “She is 57 and needs assistance to even walk around the stage.”

Another responded to a separate video of Carey—mom to 15-year-old twins Moroccan and Monroe with ex Nick Cannon—appearing to struggle to walk in high heels, “If her health really is bad then she needs to take time off and work on herself.”

But some of Carey’s Lambily quickly hopped in to defend her, even before the Grammy winner herself stepped in. As one fan joked in response to the speculation, “She’s still defrosting!!”

The comment, of course, references Carey’s reign as the queen of the festive season—but she has at least begun to thaw this Christmas by kicking off her latest tour.

“Thank you São Paulo!!” Carey wrote in a Nov. 18 post on social media, alongside picture that featured her decked out in Santa outfits. “What an incredible way to kick off my Christmastime Tour.”

And while Carey has become synonymous for her holiday hit—which was only just this year dethroned as the longest charting Billboard hit by Ella Langley’s “Choosin’ Texas”—she has previously emphasized that it isn’t a fluke and she “really, truly loves the holidays.”

“It’s not fake,” she told Good Morning America of her seasonal spirit in 2023, noting that she loves touring during the winter to ensure her fans have “the merriest of Christmases.”

Even 32 years after she put out the record, Carey isn’t surprised it remains her chart-topper, either. After all, fans being obsessed with it was the point. As she put of writing it back in the early 1990s, “My goal was to do something timeless.”

For more fascinating facts about Carey, keep reading…

1. Mariah Carey was born on March 27, 1970 (we think) to Alfred Roy Carey and Patricia Hickey, who'd been disowned by her family for marrying a man of African-American and Afro-Venezuelan descent. They divorced when Carey was 3.

2. Her name comes from the song "They Call the Wind Maria" from the 1951 Broadway musical Paint Your Wagon. Though it isn't spelled the same, it is pronounced the same.

3. When she was young, her family, which includes two older siblings, was subjected to racist attacks because of her parents' interracial marriage. Their dogs were poisoned and a car was blown up.

"That was the beginning of a nightmare," Patricia told Oprah Winfrey during a 1999 interview with her daughter. "When we were eating one night, someone fired a shot through the window. Luckily, none of the children were in the room."

4. While attending Harborfields High School in Greenlawn, New York, she earned the nickname Mirage because of how often she skipped class. According to her unauthorized 2001 biography by Marc Shapiro, Carey's truancy was of great concern for guidance counselor John Garvey.

"You could talk to her until you were blue in the face, and it didn't do any good," Garvey said in the book. "When you talked to her about it, she'd let you know it wasn't that important in her life because she was going to be a rock star." Carey's response? "I don't blame them for trying to encourage me to do better scholastically because they never saw me sing."

5. While recording her third album Music Box, she and Columbia Records head Tommy Mottola began dating. They tied the knot in a lavish ceremony in June 5, 1993. They eventually separated in 1997, with Carey citing his controlling nature and growing creative differences at the catalyst for their relationship's deterioration.

Carey opened up about the relationship on her E! reality series, Mariah's World, saying "I was with someone at the time that had a lot of control over my life. He was older than me by a lot and had a lot of power and he wanted me to remain away from most people, like sequestered. I had to get permission to leave [the house]…I never thought I would get out of there."

6. The first song she ever composed was written while she was still in high school. Called "Here We Go 'Round Again," Carey has described the track, which was featured on her four-song demo tape, as having a Motown vibe.

7. After graduating high school, she moved into a one-bedroom apartment in Manhattan that she shared with four other female students. She worked as a waitress and a coat check girl while also studying cosmetology—all while pursuing her dreams of a music career.

8. At the age of 2, Carey stunned her mom, a vocal coach and occasional opera singer, by imitating her operatic singing. She began taking singing lessons at 4, developing a voice that would span five octaves—a nearly superhuman feat.

9. After meeting rising pop singer Brenda K. Starr, Carey tagged along as her new friend attended a Christmas party hosted by CBS Records. Starr convinced her to bring along her demo tape, which made its way into the hands of the head of Columbia Records, Mottola. All it took was two songs to convince Mottola to sign her.

10. Carey's debut eponymous album was released on June 12, 1990. She earned two Grammys following its release, including one for Best New Artist, and it made her the first musical act since the Jackson 5 to have their first four singles reach number one.

11. Carey's had a life-long affection for Marilyn Monroe and, in 1999, she paid over $600,000 for the icon's white baby grand piano when it came up for auction at Christie's.

12. After meeting Nick Cannon on the set of her music video for "Bye Bye" in 2008, she began dating the comedian. Within two months, they got married, tying the knot on April 30 in a secret ceremony in the Bahamas. They split in 2014.

13. Though she suffered a miscarriage shortly after marrying Cannon, the couple would welcome twins Moroccan and Monroe in 2011. Monroe was named after Carey's idol, Marilyn Monroe, while Moroccan was named after the décor in the room where his father proposed to his mother.

14. In 2008, Carey got herself into some hot water when she performed in a New Year's Eve concert for the family of Libyan dictator Muammar Gaddafi. She would later claim to "feel horrible and embarrassed to have participated in the event," with her rep Cindi Berger promising in March 2011 that royalties for her upcoming song "Save The Day" would be donated to charities that create awareness for human rights issues. The song was eventually released in 2020.

15. In 2001, after a bizarre appearance on TRL that involved her showing up unannounced, pushing an ice cream cart, and stripping off her clothes, she spent two weeks recovering in a Connecticut mental hospital, citing extreme exhaustion for her breakdown.

She later revealed in 2018 that she had been diagnosed with bipolar II disorder at the time, but kept her diagnosis private. "I'm just in a really good place right now, where I'm comfortable discussing my struggles with bipolar II disorder," she told People at the time. "I'm hopeful we can get to a place where the stigma is lifted from people going through anything alone. It can be incredibly isolating. It does not have to define you and I refuse to allow it to define me or control me."

16. Just months after her stay in the institution, Carey released Glitter into the world. The film and its soundtrack were such bombs that they prompted Virgin Records to buy her out of her record deal for $50 million. She would sign a contract with Island Records in early 2002, launching the record label MonarC in the process.

17. Glitter isn't the only film Carey has appeared in over the years. She earned some pretty great reviews for her work as a social worker in friend Lee Daniels' award-winning 2009 film Precious. In 2013, she appeared in another Daniels' film, The Butler, playing Hattie Pearl, the mother of the film's titular butler, Cecil Gaines (played by Forest Whitaker).

18. Until 2017 "One Sweet Day," Carey's song with Boyz II Men, was the longest-running number one song in history, with 16 weeks spent atop the Billboard Hot 100 in 1995-96. It shared the honor with Luis Fonsi and Daddy Yankee's "Despacito," featuring Justin Bieber, for a time before Lil Nas X's "Old Town Road" surpassed it in the summer of 2019.

19. According to Page Six, Carey's two Jack Russell terriers only fly first class and each with their own respective staff member. Why? Because they apparently loathe one another.

20. Following a career resurgence with the 2005 release of The Emancipation of Mimi, Carey begun lending her name and likeness to endorsement deals for the first time in her career, beginning with ads for Intel Centrino personal computers. In 2006, after signing a contract with Gillette's "Legs of a Goddess" campaign, Carey reportedly insured her legs for $1 billion.

21. After meeting him through her longtime pal, director Brett Ratner, at a movie premiere in Aspen, Colo., Carey became engaged to Australian businessman James Packer in January 2016.

The billionaire, who formed the joint production company RatPac Entertainment with Ratner, proposed with a 35-carat ring. The engagement was called off by October, as both insinuated the relationship ended because of the other's issues.

22. Carey's classic 1994 album Merry Christmas has sold over 18 million copies worldwide, making it the best-selling Christmas album of all time. 25 years after its release, in late December of 2019, her beloved single "All I Want for Christmas Is You" hit No. 1 on the Billboard Hot 100 for the first time, breaking several records (including longest trip to the top) in the process. While extending her record as the solo artist with the most No. 1 singles on the Hot 100, the achievement also made her the only artist in history to have a No. 1 song in four consecutive decades.

23. Her godmother is Patti LaBelle, and the R&B icon has admitted to slapping her goddaughter around back in the day.

24. After Packer, Carey quickly rebounded with backup dancer Bryan Tanaka, the early stages of their relationship captured by Mariah's World cameras.

25. She does not know Jennifer Lopez. So stop asking her.
